Since S_m>D,\n\n    B_n*H_base < Z_1*lambda^m/(2*a*c^(m-1))\n                 =Z_1*c/(2*a)*(lambda/c)^m\n                 < (100/31)*Z_1.\n\nCombining with (8.10), EVERY trajectory in the precise class of\nSection 8 has\n\n    0<=B_n*V<(200/31)*Z_1*(25/49)^(m-3) ->0.            (9.1)\n\nIn particular the chosen static family cannot attain 1/B_n at large\ndepth, or exceed H_base by any fixed factor. This is a result about\nits actual payoff, not the weakness of a merely lower certificate.\nNo substituted balanced benchmark, benchmark DP, or evaluated ray\nconstant appears in the argument.\n\nFor comparison, H_base itself is attained on this exact word by the\nsingle usual Huber derivative g_H(x)=min(x,1), started at 1+2*S_m.\nAll queries then lie above one, the last value is S_m+1/2, and the\ntrue squared radius is (1+2*S_m)^2. Definitions, fixed instance, and the proposed result\n\nFix an integer m>=2 and tau=1/4. Define\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-tau/m,\n    c=lambda*(1+tau/m), a=beta/c,\n    t_j=1+a*c^j, 0<=j<m,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,t_j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    h=H_m=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), n=2^m-1, D=n+1.             (1)\n\nEvery child in (1) uses the SAME enclosing (m,tau). In particular,\nh_k=t_(nu_2(k+1)), where nu_2(r) is the largest nonnegative integer j\nsuch that 2^j divides the positive integer r. This identity follows\ninductively from the concatenation: adding 2^j to an integer strictly\nbetween 0 and 2^j preserves its smaller binary valuation. No recurrence\nin (1), or any trajectory below, was executed by a program.\n\nLet b=64. The vertices of T_D are all words of length at most D over\nan alphabet with b letters. The root r is the empty word, depth(v) is\nword length, and a parent p is connected to each word obtained by\nappending one letter. Orient each edge p->v away from the root.\nThere are b^l vertices and b^l incoming edges at depth l>=1, and\n\n    N=sum_(l=0)^D b^l=(b^(D+1)-1)/(b-1).                 (2)\n\nThe ambient inner product is the ordinary sum over ALL N vertices.\nDefine the positive constant and caps\n\n    kappa=b+1+2*sqrt(b)*cos(pi/(D+1))\n         =65+16*cos(pi/(D+1)),\n    delta_l=kappa*b^(-l)/h_(l-1), 1<=l<=n,\n    delta_D=b^(-n)/2.                                   (3)\n\nFor a positive real delta, set\n\n    psi_delta(s)=0,                         s<=0,\n                 s^2/2,                    0<=s<=delta,\n                 delta*s-delta^2/2,         s>=delta,\n    q_delta(s)=min(delta,max(s,0)).                       (4)\n\nThe formulas match at the endpoints, including their first derivatives.\nThis is a one-sided Huber energy: it is C^1 with Lipschitz derivative,\nas required by smooth convex optimization; C^infinity is not asserted.\nThe minimum cone allowed in TASK.md is relevant to this one-sided choice.\n\nDefine one static function and start by\n\n    F(x)=(1/kappa)*sum_(p->v) psi_(delta_depth(v))(x_p-x_v),\n    x_0=e_r,\n    x_(k+1)=x_k-h_k*grad F(x_k), 0<=k<n.                (5)\n\nHere e_r has value1 at the root and0 at every other vertex. All edges,\nincluding the depth-D edges used in the terminal value, are present\nbefore the first query. Caps depend only on depth and the frozen word.\nThere is no checkpoint selection, time compression, state reset, new\nedge insertion or appended step. The horizon-dependent finite dimension\nis enormous but legal; neither the tree nor its vectors were enumerated.\n\nThe joint conclusions to be proved are as follows. The least global\ngradient Lipschitz constant of F is exactly1, its minimum value is0,\nand its full minimum set and true initial distance are\n\n    K={z: z_p<=z_v for every edge p->v},\n    x_star=(1/N)*1,\n    R^2=dist(e_r,K)^2=1-1/N.                            (6)\n\nThe symbol 1 is the all-ones vertex vector. At EVERY physical query,\nincluding the unqueried terminal point, the actual state is\n\n    x_k(v)=b^(-k) if depth(v)=k, and0 otherwise,\n                        0<=k<=n.                       (7)\n\nThus one whole outgoing generation is saturated at each query; the\nprevious incoming generation is strictly inactive. There are n actual\nnonlinear handoffs, with n tending to infinity, rather than one local\nactivation followed by a fixed quadratic product.\n\nThe exact normalized terminal objective, using its closest minimizer, is\n\n    V_m=2*F(x_n)/R^2\n       =3*b^(1-n)/[4*kappa*(1-1/N)].                    (8)\n\nFor every L>0 the static function f=L*F has least smoothness L;\nthe physical updates h_k/L give the same states and the same payoff.\n\nWrite S=sum_(k=0)^(n-1) h_k and H_base=1/(1+2S). The original benchmark\ndefinitions are those of accepted fact04219554e41d9750:\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,\n    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n    C_j=max_(0<=i<j) Omega(C_i,C_(j-i-1)),\n    B_j=max_(0<=i<j) G(C_i,B_(j-i-1)), U_j^ZJ=1/B_j.  (9)\n\nEvery original split occurs in (9). We will prove the finite bounds\n\n    0<V_m/H_base<(9/2)*6^m*b^(1-2^m)<1,\n    0<B_n*V_m<(3/2)*16^(-n)<1,       m>=2.            (10)\n\nBoth displayed upper bounds tend to zero. The sharp asymptotic within\nthis frozen family is\n\n    b^n*V_m ->16/27.                                   (11)\n\nThese bounds concern the single witness (5). Its legality implies\nW_n(H_m)>=V_m; an upper bound on V_m gives no upper bound on W_n.\n\n## 2. Smoothness in the full vertex space, including all overlaps\n\nLet A be the oriented incidence map, (Ax)_(p->v)=x_p-x_v. Each\nq_delta in (4) is nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz. Thus F is everywhere\nfinite, convex, continuously differentiable, and\n\n    grad F(x)=(1/kappa)*A^T q(Ax),                       (12)\n\nwhere the edge coordinate of q uses that edge's fixed cap. It remains\nto compute the FULL-space norm of A, not the norm of a convenient\nsubspace or a disjoint-edge approximation.\n\nFor any vertex vector x put\n\n    U_l=(sum_(depth(v)=l) x_v^2)^(1/2), 0<=l<=D.\n\nAt depth l, each parent coordinate is repeated b times. The\nCauchy--Schwarz inequality gives\n\n    abs(sum_(edges p->v at depth l) x_p*x_v)\n       <=sqrt(b)*U_(l-1)*U_l.\n\nConsequently\n\n    ||Ax||^2 <=sum_(l=1)^D (sqrt(b)*U_(l-1)+U_l)^2\n              =||J U||^2,                              (13)\n\nwhere J is the D by (D+1) matrix with sqrt(b) in position (l,l-1),\n1 in position (l,l), and zero elsewhere, with rows1,...,D.\nThe symmetric matrix J J^T has diagonal b+1 and its two neighboring\ndiagonals equal sqrt(b). For r=1,...,D, the vector\n\n    v_l^(r)=sin(l*r*pi/(D+1)), l=1,...,D,\n\nis nonzero, has zero boundary values at l=0,D+1, and satisfies\n\n    (J J^T)v^(r)\n       =[b+1+2*sqrt(b)*cos(r*pi/(D+1))]*v^(r).           (14)\n\nThis follows by adding the sine values at l-1 and l+1. The D\neigenvalues are distinct, so the corresponding eigenvectors are\nlinearly independent and exhaust this D-dimensional symmetric matrix.\nTheir largest eigenvalue is kappa. Thus (13) proves ||A||^2<=kappa.\n\nFor equality, take the positive vector v_l=sin(l*pi/(D+1)) and let\nU=J^T v. All its entries are positive. Equation (14) gives\nJ^T J U=kappa U. Set the actual vertex coordinates to\n\n    x_v=(-1)^l*b^(-l/2)*U_l, depth(v)=l.\n\nAll adjacent coordinates have opposite signs, and within each level\ntheir magnitudes are equal. Equality holds in every bound in (13).\nThis vector therefore has ||Ax||^2=kappa||x||^2, proving\n\n    ||A||^2=kappa.                                    (15)\n\nBy (12) and the coordinatewise Lipschitz bound,\n\n    ||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||\n       <=||A||^2*||x-y||/kappa=||x-y||.\n\nThe constant1 is sharp. Choose a level-constant vector u with\nu_0=0 and u_l=u_(l-1)-delta_l/2. Every edge difference is strictly\nbetween0 and its cap. There is an open neighborhood of u where\nF is quadratic with Hessian A^T A/kappa. By (15) this Hessian has\nnorm1; a sufficiently small displacement along its top eigenvector\nstays in that neighborhood and attains gradient difference ratio1.\nEvery cap is positive and the tree is finite, so such a neighborhood\nexists even though its radius can be very small. No smoothness\ndiscount or coefficient-space norm is used in the physical updates.\n\n## 3. Symmetry reduction with the correct Euclidean metric\n\nPermuting the b children at any vertex leaves the entire static\nobjective and the initial vector invariant. Equation (12) shows that\nits gradient commutes with these permutations. Induction on the\nphysical steps therefore makes every x_k constant on each depth.\n\nFor a general level-constant state let y_l denote the value at depth l.\nThe induced inner product and energy are\n\n    <y,z>_tree=sum_(l=0)^D b^l*y_l*z_l,\n    F(y)=(1/kappa)*sum_(l=1)^D b^l*\n                         psi_(delta_l)(y_(l-1)-y_l).    (16)\n\nPut r_l=q_(delta_l)(y_(l-1)-y_l) for1<=l<=D and set r_0=r_(D+1)=0.\nAt a vertex of depth l the exact full-space gradient is\n\n    g_l=(b*r_(l+1)-r_l)/kappa, 0<=l<=D.               (17)\n\nFor the root there are b outgoing edges and no incoming edge; for\na leaf there is one incoming edge and no outgoing edge. At an interior\nvertex there is exactly one incoming edge and b outgoing edges. This\nproves (17) directly. Equivalently, differentiating (16) and dividing\nthe l-th derivative by the metric weight b^l gives (17).\n\nHence the physical reduced updates are\n\n    y_l^+=y_l-(h/kappa)*(b*r_(l+1)-r_l).               (18)\n\nUsing the ordinary unweighted gradient of (16) would insert a false\nfactor b^l. The genuinely Euclidean reduced coordinates are\nz_l=b^(l/2)*y_l. In these coordinates the claimed pulse has just\nz_k=b^(-k/2) nonzero. This already indicates why an exponentially\ngrowing number of children does not preserve the squared norm.\n\nSumming the full vertex gradient in (12) gives0, because every\nincidence row sums to0. Every actual GD update, of any step length,\ntherefore preserves the sum of the vertex coordinates. This is a\ngradient identity; no stochastic-kernel comparison is invoked.\n\n## 4. The full minimum cone and the closest initial point\n\nEvery term in F is nonnegative, and psi_delta(s)=0 holds exactly\nwhen s<=0. Its minimum set is therefore exactly K in (6), not\nmerely the line of constants. Constants belong to K, and F has\nminimum0. On a rooted tree, every z in K has z_r<=z_v for every\nvertex v, by following its path from the root.\n\nLet p=(1/N)*1. For any z in K,\n\n    <e_r-p,z-p>=z_r-(1/N)*sum_v z_v<=0.                (19)\n\nThe constant terms cancel because the coordinates of e_r-p sum to0.\nThe identity\n\n    ||e_r-z||^2=||e_r-p||^2+||z-p||^2\n                         -2<e_r-p,z-p>\n\nthen proves that p is a nearest point of the ENTIRE cone K. It is\nthe unique nearest point, since equality with z!=p is prevented\nby the strictly positive ||z-p||^2. Direct expansion gives\n\n    ||e_r-p||^2=(1-1/N)^2+(N-1)/N^2=1-1/N.\n\nIn particular R is positive, and the convenient minimizer0 would\ngive a different, larger radius. The denominator in (8) uses the\nactual value in (6). Every vertex, including all late descendants,\nhas been counted in N and in this projection.\n\n## 5. Every physical step gives a strict saturated handoff\n\nFirst check that the cap ratios needed for (7) really are below1\non the ORIGINAL hierarchy. Since m>=2 and tau=1/4,\n\n    beta>=7/8, c<=9*lambda/8<3,\n    h_k>=t_0=1+beta/c>31/24>81/64.\n\nThe last rational comparison follows from 31*64>81*24. Since\nD>=4, formula (3) gives b<kappa<81. Thus\n\n    0<kappa/(b*h_k)<1,           0<=k<n.              (20)\n\nNow prove (7) by induction on the ACTUAL physical index k. It is\nthe prescribed start at k=0. Suppose it holds at k<n and write\nA_k=b^(-k). At that state the outgoing depth-(k+1) edges have\ndifference A_k. Their fixed cap satisfies\n\n    delta_(k+1)/A_k=kappa/(b*h_k)<1.\n\nThey are therefore strictly saturated, all with clipped flux\ndelta_(k+1). If k>=1, every incoming depth-k edge has difference\n-A_k and contributes zero flux and zero energy. Every other edge\nhas zero difference and contributes zero flux and zero energy.\n\nUsing (17), the full gradient has the exact coordinates\n\n    grad F(x_k)(v)= A_k/h_k,       depth(v)=k,\n                    -A_k/(b*h_k), depth(v)=k+1,\n                    0,            all other depths.   (21)\n\nSubtracting h_k times (21) makes every depth-k coordinate zero\nand every depth-(k+1) coordinate A_k/b=b^(-(k+1)). It leaves\nall other coordinates unchanged. This proves the induction through\nk=n, without dropping any small or large physical step.\n\nAt the terminal query the only positive edge differences are those\nat depth D=n+1, equal to A_n=b^(-n). They are strictly saturated\nsince their cap is A_n/2. Thus the final value uses the actual\nstatic function, including all old and future edges already present.\n\nThis is a growing nonlinear evolution, not a quadratic sector.\nAt each segment between successive iterates, parametrized by\nx(s)=(1-s)x_k+s*x_(k+1), 0<=s<=1, an outgoing depth-(k+1)\ndifference changes as\n\n    A_k*[1-(1+1/b)*s].                                (22)\n\nIt starts above its cap, crosses the cap strictly before its zero\nat s=b/(b+1), and ends negative. The depth-(k+2) differences are\ns*A_k/b; for k<n-1 they cross their fixed positive cap at\ns=kappa/(b*h_(k+1)), which lies in (0,1) by (20). For k=n-1,\nthe corresponding depth-D differences cross their terminal cap at\ns=1/2. Consequently new saturated generations replace old ones at\nEVERY physical step, with genuine quadratic-to-linear and\nquadratic-to-inactive transitions along these segments. No added\nqueries along the segment are part of the algorithm.\n\nAll states in (7) are nonnegative and have coordinate sum1.\nThese properties are conclusions of the exact update, despite\nunbounded physical step sizes. No claim that (18) is a globally\npositive Markov operator is needed or made.\n\n## 6. Exact query values, overlap, and an actual energy increase\n\nCounting all b^(k+1) saturated outgoing edges gives, for k<n,\n\n    F(x_k)=b^(-k)*[1/h_k-kappa/(2*b*h_k^2)],\n    ||x_k||^2=b^(-k),\n    ||grad F(x_k)||^2=(1+1/b)*b^(-k)/h_k^2.           (23)\n\nThe value follows by substituting delta_(k+1) from (3) in\nb^(k+1)*[delta_(k+1)*A_k-delta_(k+1)^2/2]/kappa.\nAll other edge values are exactly zero, rather than omitted errors.\n\nBranches are not assumed independent. Consecutive gradients overlap\non their common generation. Formula (21) gives\n\n    <grad F(x_k),grad F(x_(k+1))>\n       =-b^(-k-1)/(h_k*h_(k+1))<0,\n                                     0<=k<n-1.       (24)\n\nThus the expected-orthogonality hypothesis of accepted\n0e7e78056dc6258c is not satisfied, even deterministically. It is\nnot an input to the payoff bound. Nonadjacent queried gradients\nwith index difference at least2 have disjoint supports, but this\ndoes not justify deleting the negative adjacent cross terms.\n\nThe trajectory can have increasing objective at a long step.\nFor a positive s>kappa/b define\n\n    w(s)=1/s-kappa/(2*b*s^2),\n    1/(2s)<w(s)<1/s.                                 (25)\n\nAt the largest bridge let k_c=2^(m-1)-1, d=t_(m-1).\nThe next physical step is t_0. By (23)--(25),\n\n    F(x_(k_c+1))/F(x_(k_c))\n       =(1/b)*w(t_0)/w(d)>d/(2*b*t_0).                (26)\n\nFor m>=10, beta>=7/8, c>2 and t_0<1+1/lambda<3/2 imply\n\n    d> (7/8)*2^(m-2) >= (7/8)*2^8=7*32>3*64\n      >2*b*t_0.\n\nHence the ACTUAL largest bridge strictly increases F whenever\nm>=10. The terminal argument below uses the exact values and\ndoes not rely on energy descent, continuous-time diffusion, or\nstable positive coefficients for all possible states.\n\n## 7. Terminal payoff and all original interpolation rows\n\nAt x_n each of the b^(n+1) depth-D edges contributes\n\n    psi_(A_n/2)(A_n)=A_n^2/2-A_n^2/8=3*A_n^2/8.\n\nEvery other edge has zero energy. Therefore\n\n    F(x_n)=3*b^(1-n)/(8*kappa),                        (27)\n\nwhich with (6) proves (8). The terminal gradient has value\nb*A_n/(2*kappa) on depth n, value -A_n/(2*kappa) on depth n+1,\nand0 elsewhere. In particular it has not been declared zero or\ndeleted just because it is unqueried.\n\nFor completeness, legality supplies every directed interpolation\nrow, with no support restriction. Here is a direct proof. For any\nconvex differentiable 1-smooth function J, integration of its\nLipschitz gradient along a line segment gives\n\n    J(u+v)<=J(u)+<grad J(u),v>+||v||^2/2.\n\nFix x and put J_x(z)=F(z)-<grad F(x),z>. Convexity makes x a\nglobal minimizer of J_x. Apply the preceding inequality at y with\nv=-grad J_x(y), then compare its stepped value with J_x(x). This\ngives\n\n    F(y)-F(x)-<grad F(x),y-x>\n                         >=||grad F(y)-grad F(x)||^2/2.\n\nEquivalently, for EVERY ordered pair of actual samples i,j,\nincluding i or j equal to n or the closest minimizer star,\n\n    Q_ij=F(x_i)-F(x_j)+<grad F(x_i),x_j-x_i>\n                     +||grad F(x_i)-grad F(x_j)||^2/2<=0. (28)\n\nThe optimum sample has x_star from (6), F(x_star)=0 and\ngrad F(x_star)=0. The full Gram matrices of all actual vectors\nare positive semidefinite since a quadratic form in such a Gram\nmatrix is the squared norm of the corresponding vector combination.\nThis verifies legality directly in the same normalization as\naccepted fact1252b8823bb5a290; no terminal-reduction claim is used.\n\n## 8. Same-word Huber and original all-split comparisons\n\nThe scalar two-sided Huber function with cap q=1/(1+2S),\n\n    J_q(z)=z^2/2                 if |z|<=q,\n           q*|z|-q^2/2          if |z|>=q,\n\nhas least smoothness1 and unique minimizer0. From scalar start1,\nat every query it has state 1-q*P, where P is the actually executed\nprefix sum, because\n\n    1-q*S=(1+S)/(1+2S)>q.\n\nEvery step therefore uses gradient q; no intermediate overshoot\nis skipped. Its exact normalized terminal value is\n\n    2*q-(2S+1)*q^2=1/(1+2S)=H_base.                 (29)\n\nTo compare (8) with (29), use kappa>b and R^2>1/2 to get\n\n    V_m<(3/2)*b^(-n).                                (30)\n\nThe largest hierarchy step satisfies, for m>=2,\n\n    t_(m-1)=1+beta*c^(m-2)<c^m<3^m.\n\nIndeed c>lambda>2 and beta<1 imply c^(m-2)*(c^2-beta)>1.\nAll n steps are bounded by that largest step, so S<6^m and\n1+2S<3*6^m. Combining with (30) gives the first upper bound in\n(10). For m=2 the expression is 162*b^(-3)<1. On increasing\nm by one its ratio is 6*b^(-2^m)<1/2. This proves the strict\nfinite comparison and convergence to0 without numerical evaluation.\n\nFor the ORIGINAL benchmark (9), a deliberately coarse envelope is\nsufficient and will be proved instead of replacing the all-split\nmaximum by a balanced tree. Both Omega and G are nondecreasing\nin each nonnegative argument, directly from their formulas. We\nclaim C_j<=4^j and B_j<=4^j for every nonnegative integer j.\nThis holds at j=0. If it holds at all indices below j and\n0<=i<j, both arguments of either recurrence are at most\nM=4^(j-1). Therefore\n\n    Omega(C_i,C_(j-i-1))<=Omega(M,M)=lambda*M<4*M,\n    G(C_i,B_(j-i-1))<=G(M,M)=4*M.\n\nTaking the original maxima proves the induction, and in particular\n\n    B_n<=4^n. The original all-word exact-profile problem remains OPEN.\n\nOnly accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 is used for the interpolation convention\nand, AFTER all rows have been proved, its explicit conjugate interpolant.\nOnly accepted fact5fd396eba75651e7 is used for its separately stated\nsame-word Huber/benchmark comparison. Every new calculation is proved\njointly here; earlier unverified findings are not premises.\n\n## 2. Original word, normalization, and notation\n\nFor the assigned hierarchy fix an integer m>=2 and put\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta_word=1-1/(4m),\n    c=lambda*(1+1/(4m)), a_word=beta_word/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_word*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.\n\nThe physical word is h=H_m, with true length n=2^m-1. Every child keeps\nthese SAME enclosing-m parameters. There is no terminal-step deletion or\nword compression. Since the first H_2 has three steps greater than1,\nits mass, and hence the mass of H_m, exceeds3.\n\nThe construction below in fact works for ANY positive finite word\nh=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) whose mass S=sum_(t<n)h_t is at least2. Define\n\n    P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t,\n    T_i=sum_(t<i)h_t*P_t,       0<=i<=n,\n    T=T_n, eta=T/S, M=1+2S, H_base=1/M.                 (1)\n\nThus P_0=T_0=0 and P_n=S. These are written finite sums, not executed\nrecurrences. Expanding the square of the step sum gives\n\n    2T=S^2-sum_(t<n)h_t^2,\n    0<=eta<=S/2.                                       (2)\n\nUse Euclidean space R^2 with orthonormal vectors e0,e1. Set\nx_star=g_star=0 and f_star=0; x0=e0 has norm exactly1. Smoothness is1.\nFor sampled vectors and values the original residual convention is\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.         (3)\n\nAll ordered distinct pairs i,j in {star,0,...,n} must satisfy Q_ij<=0.\nAt the end, a general advertised smoothness L>0 is obtained by using\nL*F and physical steps h_i/L. The normalized error stays2f_n.\n\n## 3. One explicit finite policy\n\nChoose an amplitude epsilon with\n\n    0<epsilon<=1/16, alpha=1-2epsilon,\n    J=1+2eta+4alpha*(S-eta),\n    D=M*alpha^2+epsilon*J,\n    sigma=alpha/D.                                    (4)\n\nIn particular 7/8<=alpha<1, J>0, D>0, and sigma>0. Define EVERY original\ngradient, including the terminal gradient, by\n\n    g_i=sigma*((1-2epsilon*P_i/S)*e0\n                         +sqrt(epsilon)*(P_i/S)*e1).   (5)\n\nLet\n\n    v=sigma*e0,\n    w=(sigma/S)*(-2epsilon*e0+sqrt(epsilon)*e1).\n\nThen g_i=v+P_i*w. Define every position by the actual GD substitution:\n\n    x_i=e0-P_i*v-T_i*w.                                (6)\n\nEquations(1),(5),(6) give x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i exactly.\nNo averaging of different functions or transcripts occurs.\n\nThe full Gram matrix of (x0,g_0,...,g_n) is the Gram of these displayed\ntwo-dimensional vectors and is therefore positive semidefinite. More\nexplicitly, write q_i=P_i/S. Its entries are\n\n    <x0,x0>=1,\n    <x0,g_i>=sigma*(1-2epsilon*q_i),\n    <g_i,g_j>=sigma^2*((1-2epsilon*q_i)*(1-2epsilon*q_j)\n                                             +epsilon*q_i*q_j). (7)\n\nThe transverse covariance is epsilon*sigma^2*q_i*q_j. It is nonzero,\npositive semidefinite, and zero at the initial query. It has strong\ncross-time correlations. Indeed all pairwise gradient inner products\nare positive, since their e0 components are at least7sigma/8 and their\ne1 components are nonnegative. This is outside the expected-orthogonality\nhypothesis of accepted0e7e78056dc6258c, without using that fact as a bound.\n\nDefine one common value array by\n\n    f_n=<g_n,x_n>-||g_n||^2/2,\n    f_i=f_n+sum_(t=i)^(n-1)\n                    (h_t/2)*<g_t+g_(t+1),g_t>, i<n.   (8)\n\nThus the exact value differences are\n\n    f_(i+1)-f_i=-(h_i/2)*<g_i+g_(i+1),g_i>.            (9)\n\nEvery value in(8) is attached to an original sample. No value is discarded\nor assigned separately for different inequality checks.\n\n## 4. The exact terminal value and the initial scale\n\nEquations(5),(6) give\n\n    g_n=sigma*(alpha*e0+sqrt(epsilon)*e1),\n    x_n=(1-sigma*S+2epsilon*sigma*eta)*e0\n                                      -sqrt(epsilon)*sigma*eta*e1.\n\nExpanding the terminal expression in(8) gives\n\n    2f_n=2sigma*alpha-sigma^2*D,                        (10)\n\nbecause\n\n    D=2alpha*S+alpha^2+epsilon\n                         +2epsilon*eta*(1-2alpha)\n     =M*alpha^2+epsilon*[1+2eta+4alpha*(S-eta)].        (11)\n\nThe choice sigma=alpha/D therefore yields\n\n    2f_n=alpha^2/D=sigma^2*D.                          (12)\n\nWe will need two elementary bounds, both finite and uniform:\n\n    D>=M*alpha^2>1,\n    1/sigma=D/alpha>=M*alpha>S+1.                      (13)\n\nFor the first, M>=5 and alpha>=7/8. For the second,\n(7/8)(2S+1)-(S+1)=(6S-1)/8>0 when S>=2.\n\nThe factor sigma is explicitly optimized, not kept at its unperturbed\nvalue. Hence the loss proved later cannot be repaired merely by retuning\nthe common scalar gradient level.\n\n## 5. Payment for EVERY finite directed interpolation row\n\nPut z=||w||^2>0 and\n\n    d=-<v,w>/z=2S/(1+4epsilon),\n    u=w/sqrt(z), r_i=<u,x_i>, k_i=<u,g_i>.             (14)\n\nHere u is a unit vector; d is an auxiliary scalar, not a physical step.\nSince epsilon<=1/16 and S>=2,\n\n    d>=8S/5, d-S>=3S/5>=6/5>1.                       (15)\n\nBy(6), the projected position increments and gradient increments are\n\n    r_(i+1)-r_i=sqrt(z)*h_i*(d-P_i)>0,\n    k_(i+1)-k_i=sqrt(z)*h_i>0.                        (16)\n\nDefine k(r) on[r_0,r_n] to be the continuous function affine on each\ninterval[r_i,r_(i+1)] and equal to k_i at r_i. Its slope on that\ninterval is exactly\n\n    1/(d-P_i) <= ell:=1/(d-S)<1.                       (17)\n\nThus k is nondecreasing and ell-Lipschitz. This is the new available\npayment. The longitudinal decrease in(5) supplies a finite scalar\ncurvature margin while the transverse component grows. No signed\nquadratic cost is dropped or left to an unknown suffix budget.\n\nThe perpendicular component b=v-<u,v>*u of g_i is independent of i.\nOn a single interval, affine integration of k and(16) gives\n\n    <b,x_(i+1)-x_i> + integral_(r_i)^(r_(i+1)) k(r)dr\n       =<(g_i+g_(i+1))/2,x_(i+1)-x_i>\n       =-(h_i/2)*<g_i+g_(i+1),g_i>.\n\nSumming these EXACT equalities and using(9), for all i<j,\n\n    f_j-f_i=<b,x_j-x_i>+integral_(r_i)^(r_j) k(r)dr.    (18)\n\nLet Delta=k_j-k_i=sqrt(z)*(P_j-P_i)>0. Since k has Lipschitz\nconstant at most ell, r_j-r_i>=Delta/ell. On the final subinterval\nof length Delta/ell one has\nk(r)-k_i >= Delta-ell*(r_j-r); elsewhere k(r)-k_i>=0.\nConsequently\n\n    integral_(r_i)^(r_j)(k(r)-k_i)dr>=Delta^2/(2ell).\n\nOn the initial subinterval of the same length,\nk_j-k(r)>=Delta-ell*(r-r_i), so also\n\n    integral_(r_i)^(r_j)(k_j-k(r))dr>=Delta^2/(2ell).\n\nThe difference g_j-g_i is parallel to u and has norm Delta. Applying\n(18) to these two area inequalities proves BOTH original residuals:\n\n    Q_ij <= -(d-S-1)*z*(P_j-P_i)^2/2 <0,\n    Q_ji <= -(d-S-1)*z*(P_j-P_i)^2/2 <0.              (19)\n\nThis proof covers every pair, however remote, and includes j=n. It is\nnot an adjacent-row test or a first-order Taylor expansion. Every term\nof the finite-amplitude policy has been retained.\n\n## 6. Payment for both optimum directions\n\nFor each finite i set\n\n    E_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2-f_i.\n\nBy(8), E_n=0 and Q_(i,star)=-E_i. To check all earlier E_i, write\nP=P_i, Tloc=T_i, h=h_i. From(6), (9), and g_(i+1)=g_i+h*w,\ndirect expansion gives\n\n    E_(i+1)-E_i\n      =h*[<w,x_i>-(1+h/2)*<w,g_i>-(h/2)*z]\n      =h*z*[d*(P+1+h/2-1/sigma)\n                           -Tloc-P-(h/2)*(P+1)].       (20)\n\nFor the second line use\n<w,e0>/z=-d/sigma, <w,v>/z=-d, and g_i=v+P*w.\nSince P+h<=S and 1/sigma>S+1 by(13), the bracket in(20) is strictly\nnegative. Thus E_i>E_(i+1), and E_n=0 implies\n\n    Q_(i,star)<0 for every i<n, Q_(n,star)=0.          (21)\n\nFor the OTHER optimum direction,\n\n    Q_(star,i)=-f_i+||g_i||^2/2.\n\nAll gradient coordinates in(5) are nonnegative, with a strictly positive\ne0 component. Equation(9) therefore shows f_i>=f_n. The gradient norm\ndecreases with P: the derivative of ||v+P*w||^2 as a written polynomial\nin P is 2z*(P-d)<0 for0<=P<=S. Hence ||g_i||^2<=||g_0||^2=sigma^2.\nUsing(12),(13) gives the uniform strict margin\n\n    f_i-||g_i||^2/2 >= (sigma^2/2)*(D-1)>0,\n    Q_(star,i) <= -(sigma^2/2)*(D-1)<0.                (22)\n\nThis includes i=n. Equations(19),(21),(22) are every original ordered\ninterpolation inequality. The initially slack optimum rows have not\nbeen omitted. The perturbation preserves their strict signs at every\nallowed finite epsilon.\n\n## 7. One static function, and the TRUE radius\n\nOnly now invoke the sufficiency construction proved in accepted\nfact1252b8823bb5a290. For y in R^2 define\n\n    K_star(y)=||y||^2/2,\n    K_i(y)=<x_i,y>-f_i+||y-g_i||^2/2, 0<=i<=n,\n    K(y)=max{K_star(y),K_0(y),...,K_n(y)},\n    F(x)=sup_(y in R^2)(<x,y>-K(y)).                  (23)\n\nThe full Gram(7) is PSD and every row has already been proved.\nThe cited accepted construction therefore gives one everywhere finite\nconvex differentiable F with1-Lipschitz gradient, interpolating ALL the\ntriples(x_i,g_i,f_i), including the terminal and the optimum triple.\nIt has F(0)=0=min F. The physical GD trajectory of this ONE function\nis exactly(6) by induction from its prescribed queried gradients.\n\nThe minimizer is actually unique. Equation(22) gives\n\n    K_i(0)=-f_i+||g_i||^2/2<0=K_star(0)\n\nfor every finite i. There are finitely many i, so continuity gives a\nneighborhood of0 on which K=K_star. Therefore the subdifferential of\nK at0 is the singleton{0}. In(23) the unique maximizing y is grad F(x);\ngrad F(x)=0 holds exactly when x belongs to that subdifferential.\nThus argmin F={0}, and\n\n    dist(x0,argmin F)=||e0||=1.                       (24)\n\nThis proves the closest-radius assertion instead of substituting a\nconvenient optimum for an unknown closer one. It also shows the advertised\nsmoothness1 is sharp: locally K(y)=||y||^2/2, hence F(x)=||x||^2/2 near0.\nFor this last claim, at sufficiently small y the differentiable K has\ngradient y; strong convexity makes that y the unique maximizer for x=y.\n\n## 8. Exact family optimum and the loss at finite amplitude\n\nKeep S,T,epsilon and the vectors(5),(6), but allow ANY positive scalar\nscale sigma and ANY common values from a legal transcript with the same\noptimum sample0. The necessary original terminal row alone gives\n\n    2f_n<=2sigma*alpha-D*sigma^2\n         =alpha^2/D-D*(sigma-alpha/D)^2.              (25)\n\nHere D depends on S,T,epsilon, not on sigma. The complete legal\nconstruction(4)--(24) attains equality at sigma=alpha/D with true radius1.\nTherefore, in the stated class with0 as its radius1 minimizer, the exact\nmaximum over sigma and all values is\n\n    V_epsilon(h)=alpha^2/(M*alpha^2+epsilon*J),\n    V_epsilon/H_base\n          =alpha^2/(alpha^2+epsilon*J/M)<1.            (26)\n\nThis class definition retains x0=e0 and the initial gradient g0=sigma e0.\nIt does not secretly optimize another minimum point or a rotated initial\ngradient. The attaining member already has the true closest minimum0.\n\nAt the fixed nonzero amplitude epsilon=1/16, alpha=7/8. From eta in[0,S/2]\nand 2-4alpha<0,\n\n    1+(2alpha+1)*S <= J <= 1+4alpha*S.\n\nThe right bound implies J/M<=2alpha because 1<=2alpha. For S>=1,\nthe left bound implies J/M>=(2alpha+2)/3; after cross multiplication\nthis assertion is equivalent to (2alpha-1)*(S-1)>=0. Thus\n\n    alpha^2/(alpha^2+2epsilon*alpha)\n       <=V_epsilon/H_base\n       <=alpha^2/(alpha^2+epsilon*(2alpha+2)/3).\n\nSubstituting the displayed rational alpha and epsilon by symbolic\nsimplification gives\n\n    7/8 <= V_(1/16)(h)/H_base(h) <=49/54<1.           (27)\n\nThese bounds concern an actual nonzero finite perturbation and hold\nuniformly in the word length; no amplitude tends to zero with m.\nThe exact gain over Huber is NEGATIVE:\n\n    V_epsilon-H_base\n       =-epsilon*J/[M*(M*alpha^2+epsilon*J)].          (28)\n\nIn particular at epsilon=1/16 the relative loss is at least5/54.\nThe full expression, including every higher power of epsilon contained\nin alpha, sigma and D, is retained in(26),(28). There is no remainder\nto estimate or extrapolation from a tangent cone.\n\nAt epsilon=0 the limiting vectors have sigma=1/M, g_i=e0/M and\nx_i=(1-P_i/M)e0. Equation(8) becomes\n\n    f_i=(1/M)*(1-P_i/M)-1/(2M^2).\n\nThis is exactly the same-word Huber transcript. All finite rows and\nall finite-to-optimum rows are then tight. The policies(4)--(8) therefore\ngive a genuine finite deformation of that transcript.\n\n## 9. Retuning the scalar correction does not rescue this class\n\nThere is a broader necessary obstruction which does NOT assume(8).\nKeep x0=e0, the same physical word and clocks, but choose any\n\n    sigma>0, 0<delta<1, nu>0,\n    g_i=sigma*((1-delta*P_i/S)*e0+sqrt(nu)*(P_i/S)*e1),\n    x_i=e0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t.                          (29)\n\nAllow all function values to be chosen freely and require the original\noptimum triple0. Set A=1-delta>0. Direct expansion of Q_(n,star)<=0 gives\n\n    2f_n <= 2sigma*A-D_gen*sigma^2,\n    D_gen=M*A^2+2delta*A*(S-eta)+nu*(1+2eta).          (30)\n\nBoth extra terms in D_gen are strictly positive. Therefore every legal\nradius1 transcript in(29), with arbitrary values, satisfies\n\n    2f_n <= A^2/D_gen <1/M.                           (31)\n\nThe equality-producing scale would be A/D_gen; its mere existence as a\nnumber is not a claim that every delta,nu is legal. Legality and equality\nwere established only for the finite subfamily delta=2epsilon, nu=epsilon\nin Sections3--7. The necessary bound(31) nevertheless rules out improvement\nthrough arbitrary positive retuning of that scalar drift, covariance size,\ncommon scale, or value array inside(29).\n\nTo see exactly where the correction matters, omit it: delta=0,nu>0.\nAt the first physical step, g0=sigma e0 and\ng1-g0=sigma*sqrt(nu)*(h0/S)e1, whereas x1-x0=-h0*sigma e0.\nFor ANY two values, summing the two original rows yields\n\n    Q_01+Q_10=||g1-g0||^2-<g1-g0,x1-x0>\n             =nu*sigma^2*h0^2/S^2>0.                 (32)\n\nThus the zero-initial transverse covariance is not itself a legal\naddition to Huber. The mean decrease really pays an otherwise impossible\nrow. For delta<0, the extra positive longitudinal contribution already\nmakes the same pair fail. The upper scope delta<1 in(29) is a substantive\nrestriction: a terminal sign reversal is NOT excluded by this argument.\n\nThis is a normalization/objective obstruction in an explicit class,\nnot a global impossibility claim. It comes from the ORIGINAL terminal\nrow with the full radius fixed, not from an unproved matrix sign,\northogonality assumption, or unavailable boundary covariance budget.\n\n## 10. Scale on the unchanged tau1/4 hierarchy\n\nFor m>=1536, accepted fact5fd396eba75651e7, Section10 and its statement,\ngives for this SAME H_m\n\n    B_(2^m-1)*H_base(H_m)<237/250,\n    lim_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1)*H_base(H_m)\n                              =Z_1/(2exp(1/4))>0.    (33)\n\nHere B_n is the original all-split Zhang--Jiang denominator, and\nZ_1=lim lambda^(-m)B_(2^m-1)>0 is its accepted unconditional ray limit.\nNo seed assumption, replacement benchmark, or executed benchmark\nrecurrence is involved. The predecessor is used ONLY for(33), not as\nan upper bound on unrestricted W_n.\n\nCombining(27),(33) proves, at every m>=1536,\n\n    B_(2^m-1)*V_(1/16)(H_m)\n                     <(49/54)*(237/250)<1.            (34)\n\nAlso\n\n    liminf B_(2^m-1)*V_(1/16)(H_m)\n                      >=(7/8)*Z_1/(2exp(1/4))>0.     (35)\n\nHence the prospective signal was not trivially diluted below the\nbenchmark scale: a fixed transverse amplitude survives at that scale.\nIts exact payment has the wrong sign for improvement, by(28),(30).\nEquations(34),(35) are sufficient quantitative scale information; no\nnew infinite-product or moment asymptotic is needed.\n\nThe original target is still whether W_n^*/U_n^ZJ tends to1, with\nW_n^*=inf_(h>=0)W_n(h) and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. The affirmative direction\nneeds a uniform all-word lower bound. The present family does not give\neven an improved lower bound on its assigned hierarchy, much less that\nseparate uniform comparison.\n\n## 11.
